
=== 1. an extrusion needs a sketch
  document: {"parameters":[],"checks":[],"settings":{"chordTolerance":0.01},"schemaVersion":6}
  toolbar:
      div.group
        button title="Prázdný dokument": "Nový"
        button: "Otevřít"
        button: "Uložit"
        button disabled: "Export STL"
        input type=file hidden
      div.group
        button title="Propojit s MCP serverem (packages/mcp): AI pak čte a mění dokument otevřený v aplikaci": "AI"
      div.group
        button disabled title="Ctrl+Z": "↶ Zpět"
        button disabled title="Ctrl+Shift+Z": "↷ Znovu"
      div.group
        button title="Nová skica na rovině nebo rovinné stěně": "Skica"
        button title="Vysunout oblasti skici": "Vysunutí"
        button title="Holes for screws, nuts and inserts at points of a sketch": "Hole"
        button title="Rovina posunutá od roviny nebo rovinné stěny": "Rovina"
        button title="Repeat extrusions that add or cut, or a body: around an axis, along a direction, or mirrored in a plane": "Pattern"
        button title="Place a stored mesh (a bought part read from an STL file) as a body": "Mesh"
      div.group
        select value="" title="Ask a query about the model"
          option selected value="": "Queries…"
          option value="interference" title="Which bodies share a volume, pair by pair. Bodies that only touch are not reported.": "Interference"
          option value="sketchDiagnosis" title="How far a sketch is determined: its degrees of freedom, the entities that can still move, and the constraints that are duplicate, redundant or in conflict.": "Diagnosis of a sketch"
          option value="constraintExplanation" title="Whether a constraint of a sketch follows from other constraints, and from which.": "Explanation of a constraint"
          option value="body" title="A body in detail: its faces with their names, surfaces and areas, and its edges with their names and shapes. The names are what references use: the plane of a sketch on a face, the projection of an edge, an entity in the arguments of another query.": "Description of a body"
          option value="catalog" title="A table of data the engine carries, such as sizes of hardware or densities of materials. A catalog fills in fields; the document stores the numbers, not a reference to the row.": "Data catalog"
          option value="measure" title="Distance and angle between two faces, edges or sketch points. Exact for planes, cylinders, cones, straight and circular edges and sketch points; the smallest distance on the mesh for every pair. The time grows with the number of triangles of the two faces.": "Measure"
          option value="section" title="What a plane cuts out of the bodies: closed loops with their areas and, for every piece of a loop, the face it lies on.": "Section"
          option value="gap" title="How close bodies are to each other: for every pair within the search distance the smallest distance between their surfaces and where it is. Two bodies ask about one pair. Bodies that touch or collide have the distance zero. Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
          option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
          option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
          option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
          option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
          option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
        select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
          option selected value="": "Export…"
          option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
          option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
          option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
          option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
          option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"
        button disabled title="Check which bodies share a volume": "Collisions"
      div.group
        button: "Zobrazit vše"
        button disabled title="Save a picture of the model as PNG: the bodies that are shown, from the camera of the view and cut as the view is; without the selection, sketches, planes and dimensions": "Save picture"
        button.active title="Zobrazit nebo skrýt roviny XY, XZ, YZ": "Základní roviny"
  timeline:
      h2: "Časová osa"
      p.hint: "Zatím prázdná. Nové prvky se objeví zde."
  panel:
      h2: "Model"
      p.hint: "Pravé tlačítko myši otáčí, kolečko přibližuje, prostřední tlačítko (nebo Shift + pravé) posouvá. Na touchpadu: Alt/Option + tažení otáčí, Alt + Shift + tažení posouvá, dva prsty přibližují. Prvek na časové ose se upravuje dvojklikem."
      h2: "Parameters (0)"
      div.constraint
        input.pname value="" placeholder="name"
        input.expr value="" placeholder="expression, e.g. 2 * a"
        button: "Add"
      p.hint: "An expression can be typed into numeric fields and dimensions instead of a number. Angles in an expression are in radians; write degrees as deg(30)."
      h2: "Checks (0)"
      div.constraint
        input.pname value="" placeholder="name"
        input.expr value="" placeholder="condition, e.g. a < b"
        button: "Add"

=== 2. the base sketch: two regions
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addFeature","feature":{"id":"s1","name":"Skica 1","suppressed":false,"type":"sketch","plane":{"kind":"base","plane":"XY"},"points":{},"radii":{},"curves":{},"constraints":{},"external":{},"solved":{"inputs":"","external":{}}},"index":0}]}) -> ok
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x5)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p1","x":40,"y":30},{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p2","x":40,"y":0},{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p3","x":0,"y":30},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c1","curve":{"type":"line","a":"origin","b":"p2"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k1","constraint":{"type":"horizontal","line":"c1"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c2","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p2","b":"p1"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k2","constraint":{"type":"vertical","line":"c2"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c3","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p1","b":"p3"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k3","constraint":{"type":"horizontal","line":"c3"}},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c4","curve":{"type":"line","a":"p3","b":"origin"}},{"type":"addConstraint","sketch":"s1","id":"k4","constraint":{"type":"vertical","line":"c4"}}]}) -> ok
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x5)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p4","x":70,"y":15},{"type":"addCurve","sketch":"s1","id":"c5","curve":{"type":"circle","center":"p4"},"radius":8}]}) -> ok
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x3)
  engine: getState("s1")
  engine: diagnoseSketch("s1")  (x2)
  engine: execute({"type":"batch","commands":[{"type":"addPoint","sketch":"s1","id":"p5","x":20,"y":15}]}) -> ok
  features: s1
  feature s1:
      {"id":"s1","name":"Skica 1","suppressed":false,"type":"sketch","plane":{"kind":"base","plane":"XY"}}
      point p1: {"x":40,"y":30}
      point p2: {"x":40,"y":0}
      point p3: {"x":0,"y":30}
      point p4: {"x":70,"y":15}
      point p5: {"x":20,"y":15}
      radius c5: 8
      curve c1: {"type":"line","a":"origin","b":"p2"}
      curve c2: {"type":"line","a":"p2","b":"p1"}
      curve c3: {"type":"line","a":"p1","b":"p3"}
      curve c4: {"type":"line","a":"p3","b":"origin"}
      curve c5: {"type":"circle","center":"p4"}
      constraint k1: {"type":"horizontal","line":"c1"}
      constraint k2: {"type":"vertical","line":"c2"}
      constraint k3: {"type":"horizontal","line":"c3"}
      constraint k4: {"type":"vertical","line":"c4"}

Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    -     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    -     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    -     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    -     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted).     -     option value="section" title="What a plane cuts out of the bodies: closed loops with their areas and, for every piece of a loop, the face it lies on.": "Section"
    -     option value="gap" title="How close bodies are to each other: for every pair within the search distance the smallest distance between their surfaces and where it is. Two bodies ask about one pair. Bodies that touch or collide have the distance zero. Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    -     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    -   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    -     option selected value="": "Export…"
    -     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    -     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    -     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    -     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    -     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"

    +     option value="measure" title="Distance and angle between two faces, edges or sketch points. Exact for planes, cylinders, cones, straight and circular edges and sketch points; the smallest distance on the mesh for every pair.     +     option value="section" title="What a plane cuts out of the bodies: closed loops with their areas and, for every piece of a loop, the face it lies on.": "Section"
    +     option value="gap" title="How close bodies are to each other: for every pair within the search distance the smallest distance between their surfaces and where it is. Two bodies ask about one pair. Bodies that touch or collide have the distance zero. Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    +     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    +     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"

    +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither.     +     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error.     +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled.     +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section).     +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    -   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    -     option selected value="": "Export…"
    -     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    -     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    -     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    -     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    -     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern).     -     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither.     -     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter.     -     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error.     -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled.     -     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features.     +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    +     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face.     +     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter.     +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error.     +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"

A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Exact for planes, cylinders, cones, straight and circular edges and sketch points; the smallest distance on the mesh for every pair.     +     option value="gap" title="How close bodies are to each other: for every pair within the search distance the smallest distance between their surfaces and where it is. Two bodies ask about one pair. Bodies that touch or collide have the distance zero. Every pair of the bodies asked about is examined, so the time grows with the number of bodies, with their triangles and with the search distance.": "Smallest gap"
    +     option value="mass" title="Mass, centre of mass and inertia of one body or of several taken as one rigid piece. A body counts with the mass or the density of its body properties; `densities` overrides them by body (a what-if in another material), and `density` serves every body that states neither. A fill factor scales the bodies that take `density`. An auxiliary body adds nothing and is listed in `skipped`; a body whose mass or density cannot be evaluated fails the query.": "Mass properties"
    +     option value="print" title="How a body prints in given orientations: its size against the bed, the area that lies on the bed and the area that overhangs, per named face. Several orientations in one call can be compared. An orientation is a direction that is up, or the planar face that lies on the bed. It does not know bridges, wall thickness or supports.": "Print check"
    +     option value="hardwareHole" title="The shape of a hole for a screw, a nut or a heat-set insert of a size, from the hardware catalogs: put `shape` and `label` of the answer into a feature of the type hole (and for an insert `depth` into its extent). The heads and the nut are the largest sizes the standards allow and nothing is added to them; room for the printer is the allowance, best a parameter. The catalogs are clearanceHoles, socketHeadScrews, countersunkScrews, hexNuts and heatSetInserts; the query `catalog` shows their rows and sources.": "Hole for a piece of hardware"
    +     option value="mechanism" title="The links and joints of the model as resolved for the state asked about: where each link stands in the pose, the numbers and the axis of each joint, and what is in error. With `beyond`, what hangs on that joint. Asked with `after: { type: "setPose", … }` it answers for a pose that is not written.": "Mechanism"
    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A printed part is a body with the use `part`, a bought part one with the use `reference` (body properties); auxiliary bodies are no parts. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none. Every copy counts, also one laid out for printing. The answer is for the model as modelled, whatever the pose.": "Parts list"
    +   select value="" title="Export the model in a format of the engine"
    +     option selected value="": "Export…"
    +     option value="stl" title="Bodies as triangle meshes in binary STL, for a slicer. One file with the chosen bodies, or with `perBody` a folder with a file per body (one printable part per file).": "STL"
    +     option value="3mf" title="Bodies as the objects of one 3MF document, for a slicer: each body is an object with its name (the name of the body properties), its ID as the part number and, when the body has a colour, a base material with that colour, named by the material of the body. One file, in millimetres, the bodies where they are modelled. The file is a ZIP, compressed when the engine has a compressor: about a fifth of the size of the STL of the same bodies; otherwise about a quarter larger than the STL.": "3MF"
    +     option value="dxf" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as a DXF drawing in millimetres, for laser cutting and drilling templates (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a CIRCLE with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed POLYLINE with lines and arcs. Outer loops are on the layer OUTER, holes on the layer HOLES. The coordinates are the x and y of the plane, as the query section reports them. One file in the form of DXF release 12.": "DXF (outline)"
    +     option value="svg" title="The outline a plane cuts out of bodies as an SVG drawing with one user unit per millimetre (the same cut as the query section). A whole circle is a `circle` with the centre and radius of its surface; every other loop is one closed `path` with lines and arcs; class `outer` or `hole`, a group per body. The y of the plane points up and that of SVG down: a point (u, v) of the plane is written (u, −v). Stroked, not filled. One file.": "SVG (outline)"
    +     option value="partsCsv" title="The parts list of the model (the query partsList) as one CSV table for a spreadsheet: a record per kind of printed part, of bought part and of hardware, with its count, material, colour, the mass of one and of all in grams, a note for a row of copies that are not in the row of their source, and the IDs of its bodies or hole features. Comma separated, UTF-8, a decimal point. One file.": "Parts list (CSV)"

    +     option value="partsList" title="What to print and what to buy: the bodies of the model as rows of parts, and the hardware of the hole features. A body and its copies made by a pattern are one row with a count while they are the same part: a mirror image is another part, and so is a copy with another material, colour, mass or name, and a body that a feature changed after the pattern (so model what every copy should have before the pattern). Hardware is counted from the labels of the hole features: one piece per hole the model has, by where the holes are (a hole through several bodies is one hole, and so are holes that continue one another on one line; a copy of a body at another place has its holes too, also one further along the same line; a hole that missed or was cut away is not counted). So the label of a hole should name the piece that goes into it, with what the catalog does not know ("M3x10 countersunk screw"), and a hole that takes no piece of its own should have none.
